rows = (i + j).ravel()
cols = np.tile(np.arange(n), n)
data = np.repeat(a, n)
A = csr_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(2n-1,n))

### Greedy edge profile seed
Function `_greedy_edge_profile(n, M, max_len=None)`:
- If M<=0 return zeros.
- q = zeros(n)
- q[0]=sqrt(M)
- For k in 1..n-1:
   S = 2*sum_{j=1}^{k-1} q[j]*q[k-j] (not including j=0? q0 term separate with root? The quadratic: q_k^2 + 2 q0 q_k + 2∑_{j=1}^{k-1} q_j q_{k-j} ≤ M. Here S excludes j=0 and j=k? q_k not yet, j=k? q_k^2. For j=1..k-1.
   Discriminant = q0^2 + M - S.
   If disc<0: q[k]=0 break?
   q[k] = -q0 + sqrt(disc). If q[k]<0 q=0.
   This maximizes q_k. But for k, if q0=sqrt(M), disc = M + M - S =2M-S. q_k nonneg. For q0=0, disc=M-S, q_k=sqrt(M-S). In general. Could also choose q0 not sqrt(M) but variable; can sweep q0 from 0 to sqrt(M). To make profiles flexible/anti-align, we can generate with parameter start q0. If q0 too small, q1 can be up to sqrt(M). But c0 = q0^2 <M (inactive). Good.
- We can **construct** a left profile and a right profile. Right profile can be reversed version of another edge profile (parameter). For same greedy, high at edge -> dot high. To reduce dot, use one profile with q0=sqrt(M) (decayed) and another with q0=0 and q1 high? But c0=0 at edge may be okay. Maybe generate profiles with alternating q0 values and mix.
- Middle fill: To make sum 1, after adding p_left and p_right_rev, add a base v = max(0, (target_M?)/?)? We can just normalize; but normalizing increases max coefficients above M target. We want actual score close. We can construct a seed, normalize, then optimize. The shape may be good.

How to implement seed from edge profiles:
  Input M perhaps set by desired score T: M=T/(2n) (target score). But seed normalized sum1: after building coefficients positive not summing 1, normalize. Normalizing scales all, max becomes M * (sum?)? Actually if raw profile has self-conv max M_raw, after dividing by S, max = M_raw/S^2. If S may not be 1. To get target M_target, choose raw scale so that S=1? We can solve scale: if q_raw has sum S0 and max conv C0 (with q_raw), scaled by 1/S0 gives max C0/S0^2. We want M_target => scale =? If set q = raw * t, sum=t S0, max=t^2 C0. Normalized sum=1 if t=1/S0, max=C0/S0^2. Cannot independently choose M. We can instead target score by choosing M in greedy such that after normalization score near target. We can search over M. For each M, build left/right profiles (zero padded) plus maybe flat middle, normalize, score. Choose best. This is parametric.

Simpler: Build raw profile with cap M, normalize, let score be whatever. Then optimize. Use multiple M options e.g., M = [0.5,0.6,0.7,0.8,1.0,1.2]/(2n) times? Since target max normalized = score/(2n). For score 1.5, M=1.5/(2n)=0.75/n. Average=1/(2n). Cap M relative to average. We can choose cap parameter C where M=C/n. C from 0.8 to 2 maybe. Greedy profile sum S depends on C. We can normalize and score. Good.

But greedy profiles with M~0.75/n: n=256 M~0.00293. q0~0.054. Sum of edge profile? Let's estimate via constant y mass √C? With C=M*n=0.75. Each edge mass maybe sqrt? Could be 0.8 each, total >1. Normalize reduces max below M? Wait if raw sum >1, normalized max = M / S^2 < M. Score =2n M/S^2. If S~1.6, score=2C/S^2=1.5/2.56=0.586 impossible below lower bound? ### Seed generation detailed
Given n, rng, given sequence, best sequence:
- uniform
- exponential left/right: exp(-α i/n), exp(-α (1-i/n)), α from [0.5,1,2,4], normalized
- sinusoid: `1 + α cos(2π(f*(i/n+φ)))`, α∈[0.2,0.5,0.8,1.0], f∈[0.5? 1,2?]. Need nonnegative; clip. Use period n+2 to avoid boundary cancellation? For i=0..n-1, choose denominator `n+1` or `n+2`. Phase φ. Also square: (base)^2 to increase contrast. Use base maybe `1 + α*sin(...)`.
- quadratic/trapezoid: `1 + α*x + β*x^2` normalized positive; could approximate not.
- edge greedy: with C values [0.8,1.0,1.2,1.5,2.0], start q0 fraction [0,0.5,1.0], maybe profile length full. Construct left profile, right profile possibly reversed of different profile. Add small middle uniform. Normalize.
- random smooth logits: base uniform*(1+ampl*lowpass noise). Use mean zero lowpass.
- resample given/best to n.
- For each seed, compute score (FFT). Sort and keep top K.

Need not too many seeds per n. Time for scoring: n=4096, FFT 8192 ~ maybe 0.002s. 100 seeds 0.2s. Fine. Generate 200 seeds for small n maybe.

Greedy profile implementation efficient:
```
def _edge_profile(n, C, q0_frac):
    M = C / n  # target cap
    q0 = q0_frac * sqrt(M)
    q = zeros(n)
    q[0]=q0
    for k in 1..min(n-1, edge_len):
       S = 2*dot(q[1:k], q[k-1:0:-1])  # j=1..k-1 q_j q_{k-j}; use np.dot(q[1:k], q[1:k][::-1])? q[k-1:0:-1] length k-1.
       disc = q0*q0 + M - S
       if disc < 0: break
       val = -q0 + sqrt(max(0,disc))
       if val <=0: break
       q[k]=val
       # stop if val < 1e-12?
    return q
```
But if q0=0, q1=sqrt(M), q2? With q0=0, S=2 q1 q? For k=2: S=0 (j=1? q1 q1? Wait formula 2∑_{j=1}^{k-1} q_j q_{k-j}; for k=2, j=1 => q1^2. So c2 = q2^2 + 2 q0 q2 + 2q1^2. q1=sqrt(M) -> S=2M; disc=M-2M<0, so q2=0. So profile length 2. To have longer, q0>0. The constant y maximizing mass might need q0≈sqrt(M)? q0 high gives long decay but q1 lower. Let's test q0=sqrt(M). q1 = -√M+√(2M)= (√2-1)√M≈0.414√M. q2: S=2q1^2≈0.343M; disc=2M-0.343M=1.657M sqrt=1.287√M; q2=0.287√M. Sum decays slowly. Good.

Full sequence for seed:
```
def _edge_seed(n, C, left_frac, right_frac, reverse_right=True, mid_frac=0.05):
   L = _edge_profile(n, C, left_frac)
   R = _edge_profile(n, C, right_frac)
   a = zeros(n)
   a[:L_nonzero] += L
   a[n - R_nonzero:] += R[::-1]  # right profile distance from right edge
   a += mid_frac * (C/n? maybe uniform small)
   return normalize(a)
```

Let's design custom line-search multiplicative:
```
def _optimize_ml(a, deadline, rng, beta_base, beta_max, start_eta=0.5):
    a=norm; n; state best
    c=conv; beta = min(beta_max, beta_base / max(c))
    p, val = softmax
    eta = 0.2
    no_improve=0
    while time:
       grad=weighted; h=grad - a*np.dot(grad,a)
       scale = np.linalg.norm(h, inf)
       if scale<1e-14: perturb
       # Try decay: new = a * exp(-eta * h / scale?) If h can negative, exp positive. If h/scale large, exp under/overflow. Limit to [-5,5].
       step = np.clip(-eta * h / scale, -3, 3)
       new = a * np.exp(step)
       new = normalize(new)
       compute val_new, score_new
       if val_new < val - 1e-15 or score_new < best_score - 1e-12:
           accept; a=new; eta=min(2, eta*1.05); no_improve=0
       else:
           eta*=0.5; if eta<1e-5 restart from best
```
But if using val with beta focused, `val_new` maybe insensitive. Use smooth objective plus hard: objective = val + μ*maxc? Actually val approximates max. Could use objective = val if beta high; if beta low, maybe use score? We can do accept if `0.5*(val_new - val) + 0.5*(score_new - score) < 0`? simpler: accept if score_new < score - tol or val_new < val - tol. But smooth val may decrease while score increases; track best_score. We can accept if val_new < val - 1e-14 OR score_new < best_score - 1e-14? To avoid getting stuck, if no smooth improvement but hard score improved, accept. However line search with hard max nonsmooth may jitter.
Maybe use objective `obj = val + 0.1 * maxc`? Since both scale ~max. But if beta high val~max; adding double. Could just use val but update beta gradually. Accept if val_new < val - 1e-15. But if val flat and score improves? val includes max. Good.
Need choose beta. If beta too low, may optimize average not max. We can anneal beta slowly. But if beta too high, p too sparse, val close max, line search may reduce current max but other peaks rise.LSE with high beta and line search handles sequential max (like active set), eventually plateau. Need animate beta from low to high. For target, high beta beneficial.

Let's derive scale of h. For uniform a=1/n, c triangular, max ~1/n. beta maybe 30/max ~30n, c-max differences O(1/n), beta diff O(30), p moderate. grad h magnitude O(1/n). Scale inf ~1/n. step -eta h/scale O(eta). good.

Implement `_smooth_grad` that returns h (z-gradient) and val.

### Add perturb/restarts
In long optimization, if no value improvement for `stall` iterations or score > best*(1+gap), perturb:
- Choose one of:
   * mirror step from best with random direction: `a = normalize(best * exp(σ * lowpass_noise))`, σ small/large.
   * block multiplicative perturb.
   * mix with a new seed (best resampled or sinusoidal).
   * if LP available, use LP direction.
Restart beta lower to escape.

Track `last_improve_val`, `last_improve_hard`. If hard score improves global, update.

### Multi-start orchestration

```
def propose_candidate(seed=0, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget-40
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    given=_get_given_sequence()
    best_a=None; best_score=inf
    # quick fallbacks
    for n in [128,256,512]:
       a=make_exponential(n,1); score=score; update
    # Stage by n
    lengths = [128,256,512,1024,2048,4096]
    # allocate time proportional to length maybe.
    # For each n:
       if time left < 60 break
       seeds = generate_seeds(n, rng, given, best_a, max 60)
       scores = [score_of(s)]
       sort; choose top m = min(5, ...)
       for rank, idx:
          if time < min 30 break
          # allocate per seed: for small n 30-60s, medium 60-120, large 120-240
          t_end = min(deadline-10, now + per_time)
          res = optimize_ml(seeds[idx], t_end, rng, beta settings based n)
          update
       # after stage, if improvement, resample best to next length as seeds (already included in next generate because best passed)
    # final polish: if time left, optimize best_a for remaining
```

### LP direction more detail
We can incorporate `_lp_improve(a, deadline, rng)`:
- Only if n<=600 and scipy available and time left >30 and maybe score > target.
- Compute c, M=maxc * 0.99? Current max. Build sparse C.
- To avoid unbounded, maybe use variable g with bounds (0, 10.0/n?) Wait g will be normalized later. If bound too low, shape maybe truncated. Since a is normalized sum1, and M~1/n. In feasible cone, what max individual g_i can be? Consider row i (k=i) coefficient a0*g_i plus maybe others; if a0 small, not bound. Row n-1+i? coefficient a_{n-1}? If both endpoints small, internal g may be unbounded? But other rows with positive a constrain. For any i, g_i appears in row 2i with coefficient a_i? If a_i positive, row 2i includes a_i*g_i? Convolution row 2i: pair (i,i) term a_i g_i. Coefficient a_i. So a_i*g_i ≤ M -> g_i ≤ M/a_i. If a_i can be very small (1e-6), bound huge. But g shape with huge i maybe not useful because rescale. Bound 1000 enough. Use `ub = 1000.0`.

```
def _build_conv_matrix(a):
    n=a.size; m=2*n-1
    i = np.arange(n)
    # columns repeated and rows = i+j
    rows = np.empty(n*n, dtype=np.int32?) maybe int64. For n=600, 360k; int32 enough (max 1199). Use int32 to save.
    for? vectorized:
       base = np.repeat(i, n)  # column
       rows = np.tile(np.arange(n), n) + base[:,None]? Actually columns j? Let's do:
       col_idx = np.tile(np.arange(n), n)  # for each column, n rows? Need rows for each column: row = col + d, d=0..n-1. So if col_idx is columns repeated n, rows = col_idx + np.repeat(np.arange(n), n). Good.
       col_idx = np.repeat(np.arange(n), n)  # each column appears n times? If concatenate for col=0 rows 0..n-1, col=1 rows1..n. So col_idx = np.repeat(arange(n), n)
       offsets = np.tile(arange(n), n)
       rows = (col_idx + offsets).astype(np.int32)
       cols = col_idx.astype(np.int32)
       data = np.repeat(a, n)  # column j has a_0..a_{n-1}
    A = csr_matrix((data, (rows, cols)), shape=(m,n), dtype=np.float64)
```

Pseudo-code for `_optimize_iter(a, deadline, rng, beta_base, beta_max, mirror=True, use_active=True, active_frac=0.2, perturb_every=...)`:

```
def _optimize_iter(a, deadline, rng, beta_base=8.0, beta_max=200.0, max_stall=800):
    a = _normalize(a); n=a.size
    if n<4: a=_make_uniform(64); n=64
    bfft = _next_pow2(2*n-1); wfft = _next_pow2(3*n-2)
    c = _conv(a,bfft)
    c = np.maximum(c,0)
    maxc = float(c.max()); score = 2*n*maxc
    best = a.copy(); best_score=score; best_val=val? compute val
    # beta_coeff starts beta_base, anneals to beta_max
    beta_c = float(beta_base)
    # To make optimizer more stable, initialize a with tiny floor? a already positive maybe.
    eta = 0.35; fail=0; it=0; last_val_improve=0; last_hard_improve=0

    unif_w = np.full(2*n-1, 1.0/(2*n-1)) maybe expensive to allocate in _weights each? Precompute once.
    while time < deadline:
       maxc=max(c); if maxc<1e-300: maxc=1e-300

       p_smooth, val = _weights_lse(c, beta, scale?) # p normalized

              low = float(np.percentile(c, 10)) maybe expensive; use c.min? c.min often 0. Use max - active_rho*(max - float(np.min(c)))? min may 0, threshold could high; active count small. Use coefficient of variation: threshold = max - 0.15*max? Since all nonneg. Use thresh = max*(1-active_thr) where active_thr from 0.2 to 0.05.
              active = c >= thresh
              if active_count >0:
                 p_active = zeros; p_active[active]=1/count
                 p = active_mix*p_active + (1-active_mix)*p_smooth
           # active_mix maybe 0.35
       grad = _weighted_gradient(a,p,wfft)
       h = grad - a * float(np.dot(a, grad))
       hc = h - np.mean(h)? z-gradient already; if using multiplicative, no need center? For mirror, h should be gradient wrt z = grad - a*sum grad. It can have nonzero mean; no constraint on z constant because softmax invariant? Actually if we add constant to z, no effect, but optimization should not waste step in invariant direction. Subtract mean of h? For gradient on quotient, the invariant direction is all ones: if z + const, same a. The gradient of a smooth invariant objective should be orthogonal to ones? Let's check h = ∂S/∂z. Since S invariant under z+c1, derivative dot ones =0? For softmax: ∂S/∂z_i = g_i - a_i Σa g. Sum over i: Σg_i - Σa_iΣa g =? Not necessarily zero? Wait S(a) with a softmax(z). dS/dz_i = ∂S/∂a_i * da_i/dz_i + ∂? da_i/dz_i = a_i(δ_ij - a_j). So ∂S/∂z_i = g_i a_i - a_i Σ_j g_j a_j = a_i (g_i - μ). The gradient h_i (not multiplied by a_i) is g_i - μ. Sum over i of h_i not zero; dot a =? Σa_i h_i = Σa g - μ = μ - μ=0. But invariant direction is constant vector c: dot with h? If we move z by c1, change objective first-order = c Σ_i h_i. For invariance, Σ_i h_i should be 0? But derivative of S under z+c1 is 0 exactly, so first-order should vanish for any c: Σ h_i=0. Does our formula miss factor? Let's re-derive: S(z)=S(a(z)). ∂a_j/∂z_i = a_i(δ_ji - a_j). ∂S/∂z_i = Σ_j ∂S/∂a_j ∂a_j/∂z_i = Σ_j g_j a_i(δ_ji - a_j) = a_i g_i - a_i Σ_j g_j a_j. This is soft gradient, not h_i. The vector of z gradients is a_i*(g_i-μ). If we do multiplicative update a_i ∝ exp(-η h_i) where h_i = g_i-μ, that's equivalent to z update z_i -= η (g_i-μ). But actual gradient in z is a_i(g_i-μ); a_i factor. Mirror descent uses h_i = derivative of S with respect to log a (which is g_i - μ), without a_i factor? Let's check: a_i = exp z_i /Z. dS/dlog a_i? If changing log a_i while not renormalizing? For constrained with sum, perhaps. But for softmax, z update should use soft gradient `sg_i = a_i*(g_i-μ)`. Multiplicative update a_i <- a_i exp(-η (g_i-μ)) corresponds to using h=g_i-μ, not gradient w.r.t z; it is mirror descent on measure, not Euclidean in z. It can be good. The invariant direction? We can optionally subtract mean_h = μ? μ=dot(a,g); h already mean weighted zero. The average of h not zero but constant update scales all a, normalized unchanged. So okay.
       scale = max(abs(h)); if small: perturb

       accepted=False

          accept if val_new < val - 1e-14 or (new_score < score - 1e-12)
          if accept: update a,c,score; break
       if not accept:

          if eta<1e-5: perturb/reset

       if fail > restart: perturb.
       it++
    return best, best_score
```

```
def _lse_p(c, beta):
    cmax = c.max()
    if beta <= 0: p=uniform; val=cmax
    else:
       beta = min(beta, 500? wait if max small)
       x = beta*(c-cmax)
       x = np.clip(x, -745, 0)
       e=exp(x); s=e.sum(); p=e/s; val=cmax + log(s)/beta
    return p, val
```

`_edge_profile(n, C, q0_frac)`:
```
M=C/n
q=zeros(n)
q0 = q0_frac*np.sqrt(M)
q[0]=q0
max_len = min(n, 4*int(np.sqrt(n))+100)
for k in range(1,max_len):
    if k==1: s=0
    else:
       # q[1:k] dot q[k-1:0:-1] reversed (length k-1)
       seg = q[1:k]
       s = 2.0 * float(np.dot(seg, seg[::-1]))
    disc = q0*q0 + M - s
    if disc < 0: break
    val = -q0 + math.sqrt(max(0,disc))
    if val <= 1e-15: break
    q[k]=val
# return q, count last nonzero+1
```

#### Gradient wrt a with weights p of length 2n-1
Our `_weighted_gradient` expects weights length 2n-1. It uses `ar=a[::-1]`; `conv = irfft(rfft(ar)*rfft(weights))`; returns `conv[n-1:2n-1]`. Let's test n=3. a=(a0,a1,a2), weights w0..w4.
ar=(a2,a1,a0). Linear conv ar*w length 5? Actually lengths 3,5 ->7. conv indices:
0 a2 w0
1 a2 w1 + a1 w0
2 a2 w2 + a1 w1 + a0 w0
3 a2 w3 + a1 w2 + a0 w1
4 a2 w4 + a1 w3 + a0 w2
5 a1 w4 + a0 w3
6 a0 w4
Slice [2:5] (n-1=2 to 4): [
a2w2+a1w1+a0w0,
a2w3+a1w2+a0w1,
a2w4+a1w3+a0w2]
We want grad_i=2∑_k w_k a_{k-i}.
i=0: 2(sum w0 a0 + w1 a1 + w2 a2 + w3 a3? invalid + w4?) =2(w0a0+w1a1+w2a2+w3a? k=3: a3 invalid; k4 a4 invalid) => 2(w0a0+w1a1+w2a2). Our slice i=0? first element = a2w2+a1w1+a0w0 = w0a0+w1a1+w2a2. good.
i=1: 2∑ w_k a_{k-1}: k=1 w1a0 + k2 w2a1 + k3 w3a2 + k? ->2(w1a0+w2a1+w3a2). slice second = a0w1+a1w2+a2w3. good.
i=2: 2(w2a0+w3a1+w4a2). slice third = a0w2+a1w3+a2w4. good. So correct.

#### Weights mix active

If beta high, lse_old often identical max; if current max not reduced but another peak reduced, lse no change? If max remains same, lse_new may be slightly lower if second? LSE includes second via exp; okay. If p active one-hot and max unchanged, lse unchanged, but score may improve if max reduced? If max unchanged no. If max reduced, lse reduces. Good.

Need ensure `p` for gradient maybe mixed active, but lse for acceptance soft. Fine.

#### Perturbations
Functions:
```
def _perturb_mirror(a, rng, sigma=0.1):
    noise = rng.normal(size=a.size)
    # lowpass? optional: if want global, no
    if use lowpass: maybe `sigma_low`
    a2 = a * np.exp(sigma*noise)
    return normalize(a2)
```
But random white noise high frequency may create spikes; use mix: 50% block, 20% lowpass, 30% white. For local escape.
`_block_perturb(a, rng, width, strength)`: pick interval, multiply exp normal.
`_mix_perturb(a, b, t)`: normalize(t*a + (1-t)*b).

In optimizer, on stall:
```
mode = rng.random()
if mode<0.3: a = _perturb_mirror(best, rng, 0.25)
elif mode<0.5: a = _block_perturb(best, rng, 0.3)
elif mode<0.7: a = _mix_perturb(best, _make_exp(n,1), 0.2)
else: a = _make_random_smooth(n,rng,1,0.5)
```
Reset eta, beta etc.

#### Optimizer returns best and best_score. If candidate goes weird, normalize.

#### `_norm` efficient
```
def _norm(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0: return np.array([1.0])
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)/a.size
    return a/s
```
Using `np.where` each time maybe. In optim loops finite, can use:
```
def _normalize_fast(a):
   a=np.maximum(a,0.0); s=a.sum(); ...
```
Need convert to array. Use one for seeds, one for inner? Simpler one.

#### `_conv`
```
def _conv(a, fft_size=None):
    n = a.size
    m=2*n-1
    if fft_size is None: fft_size=_next_pow2(m)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, fft_size)
    b=np.fft.irfft(A*A, fft_size)
    b=b[:m]
    # clip small negative
    if b.size:
       # Use np.maximum only if min<0? to avoid allocation?
       if np.min(b) < 0: b=np.maximum(b,0.0)
    return b
```
`np.min` cost m; but we need max later. Could combine:
```
b=np.maximum(b,0.0); return b
```
Allocation okay. In inner loop we also need max. We can return c and max? `_conv_max(a,fft)` returns c,max. But weights need c. Use `_conv` then `maxc=c.max()`.

#### `_grad`
```
def _grad(a, weights, fft_size):
    ar = a[::-1]
    A = np.fft.rfft(ar, fft_size)
    W = np.fft.rfft(weights, fft_size)
    s = np.fft.irfft(A*W, fft_size)
    n=a.size
    g = s[n-1:2*n-1]
    g = (g + g[::-1])? No gradient not symmetric. Just real.
    np.maximum(g,0?) Gradient can have positive; no need nonnegative. But due roundoff maybe. Return 2*g.
```
Since weights nonnegative and a nonnegative, g nonnegative theoretically. Clip negative to 0.
```
g = np.maximum(g,0)
return 2*g
```

#### `_lse_p`
```
def _lse_p(c, beta):
    cmax = float(c.max())
    m=c.size
    if beta <= 0 or cmax <=0:
       return np.full(m,1/m), cmax
    beta = min(float(beta), 1e7)
    x = beta*(c-cmax)
    x = np.clip(x, -745.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(x)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0: return np.full(m,1/m), cmax
    p=e/s
    val = cmax + math.log(s)/beta
    return p, float(val)
```
If beta huge and cmax repeated, s=count, val=cmax+log(count)/beta ~cmax.

#### `_mix_weights`
```
def _mix_weights(c, p_soft, active_rho, active_mix):
    m=c.size
    if active_mix <=0 and active_rho<=0:
       p = p_soft
    else:
       maxc = float(c.max()); minc = float(c.min())
       range = maxc - minc
       if range <= 0: thresh=maxc
       else: thresh = maxc - float(active_rho)*range
       active = c >= thresh
       cnt=int(active.sum())
       if cnt > 1:
          p_active = np.zeros(m)
          p_active[active]=1.0/cnt
          p = (1.0 - active_mix)*p_soft + active_mix*p_active
       else: p=p_soft
    # Add tiny uniform
    p = 0.999*p + 0.001*(1.0/m)
    s=p.sum(); if s>0: p/=s
    return p
```
If active_rho=0, all active; p_active uniform over all, p becomes uniform; bad. Use only if active_rho>0. The `0.001` maybe too much? It slightly smooths. For n large 0.001 negligible. Could use 1e-6. Use `base=1e-6`.

#### `_make_eval_seed` include `mid_factor` etc.

```
def _seed_x(n): return np.linspace(0,1,n)
```
No need.

`_make_lin(n, slope)`:
```
x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
a=1.0 + slope*(2*x-1)
a=np.maximum(a,1e-6)
return _norm(a)
```

`_make_sin(n, amp, freq, phase, square)`:
```
x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
a=1 + amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*(freq*x+phase))
if square: a=a*a
a=np.maximum(a,1e-6); return _norm(a)
```

`_make_rand_smooth(n,rng,amp,cutoff_factor?)`:
```
z=rng.normal(n)
F=np.fft.rfft(z)
cut=max(2, len(F)//max(1,cutoff_factor))
if cut<len(F): F[cut:]=0
f=np.fft.irfft(F,n)
f=f-f.mean(); sd=f.std(); if sd>0: f/=sd
a=1+amp*f; a=np.maximum(a,1e-6); return _norm(a)
```
For multiple random seeds, use different cutoff.

##### Edge seed
Need `_profile_count` maybe return q and cnt. Simpler return q full.
```
def _edge_profile(n,C,q0_frac):
    M=C/n
    q=np.zeros(n)
    if M<=0: return q
    q0 = q0_frac*math.sqrt(M)
    q[0]=q0
    max_len = min(n, 4*int(math.sqrt(n))+100)
    for k in range(1,max_len):
       if k == 1:
          s=0.0
       else:
          seg=q[1:k]
          # reverse view? np.dot(seg, seg[::-1])
          s=2.0*float(np.dot(seg, seg[::-1]))
       disc = q0*q0 + M - s
       if disc <=0: break
       val = -q0 + math.sqrt(disc)
       if val <= 1e-14: break
       q[k]=val
    return q
```
This is O(k^2) with dot allocations each loop. For many seeds, could be slow. We can optimize by using scalar sum loop? Dot vectorized faster but alloc seg each k. For L~100, fine. For 100 profiles n=4096, 100*100*100 ~1M? okay. If L ~500, 12.5M maybe okay.
But profile with q0_frac=0: q[0]=0, q[1]=sqrt(M), then break at k=2. good.
`q0_frac=0` q[0]=0; max_len loop k=1, disc=M, val=sqrt(M), q1. k=2, s=2*q1^2=2M, disc=0+M-2M=-M break. Good.

`_make_edge_seed(n,C,lf,rf,mid)`:
```
L=_edge_profile(n,C,lf); R=_edge_profile(n,C,rf)
a=np.zeros(n)
cntL=int(np.count_nonzero(L)); if cntL: a[:cntL]=L
cntR=int(np.count_nonzero(R)); if cntR: a[n-cntR:]=R[:cntR][::-1]
if mid >0:
   a += mid * (C/n)
# add small floor to all? a += 1e-6*(C/n)
return _norm(a)
```
If mid mass too high, normalize.

##### Sparse seed
```
def _make_sparse(n,m,rng,edge_frac=0.0):
    a=np.zeros(n)
    if m <=0: return uniform
    if edge_frac>0:
       L=max(2,int(n*edge_frac)); m1=m//2; m2=m-m1
       left=np.unique(rng.integers(0,L,m1)); right=n-1-np.unique(rng.integers(0,L,m2))
       support=np.unique(np.concatenate([left,right]))
    else:
       support=np.unique(rng.integers(0,n,m))
    a[support]=1.0
    if rng.random()<0.5:
       w=rng.dirichlet(np.ones(len(support))) * len(support)? Maybe assign random weights equal-ish.
       a[support]=w
    a += 1e-6
    return _norm(a)
```
Dirichlet can introduce very small values; okay.

##### Resample
```
def _resample(old,n,mode):
 old=_norm(old); # on [0,1]
 if old.size==n: return old.copy()
 x_old=np.linspace(0,1,old.size)
 x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
 if mode<0.3:
    y=np.interp(x_new,x_old,old)
 elif mode<0.6:
    y=np.exp(np.interp(x_new,x_old,np.log(old+1e-9)))
 else:
    y=np.interp(x_new,x_old,old)
    y=y*np.exp(0.5*(1-y.max()))? no
 a=np.maximum(y,1e-9)
 return _norm(a)
```
Also `mode=2` periodic? Not.

##### Seed collection
```
def _make_seeds(n,rng,given,best):
    seeds=[]
    def add(a): seeds.append(_norm(a))
    seeds.append(_make_uniform(n))
    for alpha in (0.5,1,2,4): add(_make_exp(n,alpha)); add(_make_exp(n,alpha,flip=True))
    for slope in (-1,-0.5,0.5,1): add(_make_lin(n,slope))
    # sin
    for amp in (0.3,0.6,0.9):
      for freq in (0.5,1,2):
        for phase in (0,0.25,0.5): add(_make_sin(...))
    # random smooth 8
    for amp in (0.25,0.5,0.8): ...
    # edge
    for C in (0.8,1.0,1.3,1.7,2.5):
      for lf in (0.0,0.4,1.0):
        for rf in (0.0,0.4,1.0):
          for mid in (0.0,0.3,1.0): add(_make_edge_seed(...))
    # sparse
    for m_factor in (0.7,1.0,1.5,2.5):
       m=int(m_factor*math.sqrt(n)); m=min(max(2,m),n)
       add(_make_sparse(n,m,rng)); add(edge_frac=0.2)
    if given: for mode in (0,1,2): add(_resample(given,n,mode))
    if best: for mode in (0,1,2): add(_resample(best,n,mode)); mix with exp
    # cap? maybe if len seeds >250, random sample?
    # Remove exact duplicates? not necessary; score sort.
    return seeds
```
This can be >300 seeds; scoring 300*n up to 4096 300*0.005=1.5s; okay. For many stages maybe 5s. But generate edge O(L^2) 15*9=135 profiles per n; for n=4096 135*~10k=1.35M dot overhead; okay.
But random smooth 8; total ~200.
Need avoid too many memory/time. Could cap seeds to 160 by random sampling if len>160? But deterministic edge/sin important. We can score all? For n=8192, edge L~160, 135*25k=3.4M; fine; FFT 160*0.01=1.6s. okay. But top optimization limited. We can let all.

```
if len(seeds)>120:

```

Maybe code seed categories and choose.

Score seeds:
```
def _score_seeds(seeds):
   scored=[]
   for a in seeds:
      a=_norm(a)
      c=_conv(a); sc=2*n*c.max()
      if np.isfinite(sc): scored.append((sc,a))
   scored.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
```
No lambdas? The rule says no lambda functions. Avoid lambdas. Use `scored.sort(key= _scored_key)` top-level or manual. Define `_score_key(item): return item[0]`. But no lambdas. Use `scored.sort(key=_item_score)`. Top-level `_item_score`.
Or sort by list of indices: `order=np.argsort([s for s,a in scored])`; but comprehension okay no lambda? It's a comprehension, not lambda. But no lambda specifically. Use built-in sort with key may require function. Define top-level `_first(x): return x[0]`.

Need avoid generator expressions? They are fine? Rule no lambda only? It says "Don't use any lambda functions." Generator is okay? To be safe, no lambdas. Comprehensions okay.

#### `_optimize_once` full code
```
def _optimize_once(a, seconds, rng, profile=0):
    start=time.monotonic()
    deadline=start+max(seconds,0.0)
    a=_norm(a)
    n=a.size
    if n<8: a=_make_uniform(64); n=64
    if n>16384: a=_resample(a,8192,0); n=8192
    bfft=_next_pow2(2*n-1); wfft=_next_pow2(3*n-2)
    c=_conv(a,bfft); maxc=float(c.max()); score=2*n*maxc
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score
    # profiles dict: (beta_start,beta_end,active_rho,active_mix,eta_initial)
    if profile==0: beta_start=4; beta_end=25; arho=0.25; amix=0.3; eta0=0.35
    elif profile==1: beta_start=8; beta_end=70; arho=0.08; amix=0.15; eta0=0.25
    elif profile==2: beta_start=3; beta_end=15; arho=0.35; amix=0.45; eta0=0.45
    else: rng
    beta=beta_start
    eta=eta0; fail=0; it=0; last_val=0; last_hard=0; best_val=None
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       if maxc <=0: maxc=1e-300
       beta_use = min(1e7, beta / maxc)
       p_soft, lse_old = _lse_p(c, beta_use)
       p = _mix_weights(c,p_soft,arho,amix)
       grad = _grad(a,p,wfft)
       mu = float(np.dot(a, grad))
       h = grad - a*mu
       scale = float(np.max(np.abs(h))) if h.size else 0.0
       if not np.isfinite(scale) or scale < 1e-14:
           # perturb
           a = _perturb_mix(best, rng, 0.25)
           c=_conv(a,bfft); maxc=float(c.max()); score=2*n*maxc
           eta=eta0; fail=0; beta=max(beta_start,beta*0.7); continue
       step_vec = -eta*h/scale
       step_vec = np.clip(step_vec, -4.0, 4.0)
       new = a*np.exp(step_vec)
       new = _norm(new)
       c_new = _conv(new,bfft)
       maxc_new=float(c_new.max()); score_new=2*n*maxc_new
       _, lse_new = _lse_p(c_new,beta_use)
       accepted=False
       if new_score_is_better:
          accepted=True
       if accepted:
          a=new; c=c_new; maxc=maxc_new; score=score_new
          eta=min(2.0, eta*1.06); fail=0
          if score < best_score - 1e-14:
             best=a.copy(); best_score=score; last_hard=it
       else:
          eta *=0.5
          fail +=1
          if eta < 1e-5 or fail > 200:
             # restart
             mode=rng.random()
             if mode<0.35:
                a=_perturb_mix(best,rng,0.3)
             elif mode<0.6:
                a=_resample(best, n, int(rng.integers(0,3)))? same n, not helpful
                a=_perturb_mix(best,rng,0.5)
             else:
                a=_make_rand_smooth(n,rng,0.5) or _mix best/exp
             c=_conv(a,bfft); maxc=float(c.max()); score=2*n*maxc
             eta=eta0; fail=0; beta=max(beta_start, beta*0.5)
       # anneal beta
       if it % 50 == 0:
          # Move towards beta_end slowly
          if beta < beta_end:
             beta = min(beta_end, beta * 1.03)
          else:
             beta = max(beta_start, beta * 0.97)
       # If score much worse than best, restart
       if fail > 800 and score > best_score + 1e-3:
          a = _perturb_mix(best,rng,0.35)
          c=... reset
       it +=1
    return best, best_score
```
Acceptance condition: Use `lse_new < lse_old - 1e-14` or `score_new < best_score - 1e-14`. But if score_new < current score but not best, accept to progress? Use `score_new < score - 1e-12` or `score_new < best_score -1e-14`. Also if `lse_new` not better but `score_new` slightly lower than current. Let's set:
```
hard_improve = score_new < score - 1e-12 or score_new < best_score - 1e-14
smooth_improve = lse_new < lse_old - 1e-14
if hard_improve or smooth_improve: accept
```
But lse_old/new with high beta may be same due max unchanged, while `score_new` = current max maybe same. If moving reduces a nonmax coefficient, no accept; but might be preparing. However LSE includes nonmax if r near; okay. If active mix, maybe. We could also accept if `new.pop`? no.
Use `score_new < best_score - 1e-13` for exploit.

Need avoid infinite loop with tiny progress. Track last_val_improve based on lse; if no lse improve for 1000 iterations, perturb.
```
if lse_new < best_lse - 1e-14: best_lse=lse_new; last_val=it
```
But best_lse point may not have best score. Use for stall: `if it-last_val>800 and it-last_hard>800`: perturb. Or if fail>300.

`_perturb_mix(a,rng,sigma)`:
```
def _perturb_mix(a, rng, sigma):
    r=rng.random()
    a=np.asarray(a)
    if r<0.35:
       w=max(2,a.size//32)
       start=int(rng.integers(0,max(1,a.size-w)))
       seg=slice(start,start+w)
       a2=a.copy(); a2[seg]*=np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma))
       if rng.random()<0.3: a2=0.5*(a2+a2[::-1])
    elif r<0.7:
       noise=rng.normal(0,sigma,a.size)
       # lowpass maybe:
       a2=a*np.exp(noise)
    else:
       m=int(a.size//max(1,16))
       a2=a.copy()
       # zero out random block? 
       start=rng.integers(0,max(1,a.size-m))
       a2[start:start+m] *= 0.5
    return _norm(a2)
```
Symmetrizing may hurt because optimal asymmetric; avoid 50% random. Could symmetrize rarely. Actually optimal likely asymmetric, so symmetrizing bad. Maybe don't sym. Use block perturb.

#### SLSQP polish
Top-level `_SLS = {...}` and functions.
Need be careful `args` to minimize: `_sls_objective` signature `(x, beta)`. `_sls_gradient` `(x, beta)`. Constraint top-level `_sls_sum(x): return x.sum()-1`.
Bounds list creation `[(0.0,1.0)]*n` allowed? It creates same tuple repeated but tuples immutable okay.
```
def _polish_slsqp(a, seconds, rng):
   if not HAVE_SCIPY: return a, _score(a)
   n=a.size
   if n>512: return a,_score(a)
   deadline=time.monotonic()+seconds
   a=_norm(a); best=a; best_score=_score(a)
   for beta in (3,8,16,32):
      if time.monotonic()>deadline: break
      _SLS['beta']=beta; _SLS['active_rho']=...; _SLS['active_mix']=...
      try:
         res=_sp_opt.minimize(_sls_objective, a, jac=_sls_gradient, method='SLSQP',
                   bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n, args=(beta,),
                   constraints=[{'type':'eq','fun':_sls_sum}],
                   options={'maxiter':80, 'ftol':1e-10, 'disp':False})
         if res.x is not None:
             cand=_norm(res.x)
             sc=_score(cand)
             if sc<best_score: best=cand; best_score=sc
             a=cand
      except Exception:
         pass
   return best,best_score
```

```
def _sls_objective(x,beta):
    a=_norm(x)
    c=_conv(a,_SLS['bfft'])
    maxc=c.max()
    if maxc<=0: return 1e100
    p_soft,val=_lse_p(c, min(1e7,beta/maxc))
    p=_mix_weights(c,p_soft,_SLS['active_rho'],_SLS['active_mix'])
    return float(p.dot(c))
```

```
   p=_mix_weights(...)

```

Potential issue SLSQP with objective p.dot(c) where p depends on c (endogenous) -> not smooth; gradient ignores derivative of p. LSE gradient is smooth; mixing with active uniform non-smooth at threshold. Could make SLSQP fail. Use p_soft only for SLSQP (no active) to be smoother. Set active_mix=0. Objective LSE val; gradient LSE (including derivative of p) which we compute? Our `_grad` with p is only ∂(∑p c)/∂a, not derivative of p. For LSE, derivative of LSE is p * ∂c/∂a /? Wait LSE = m + log(∑e^{βc})/β. Derivative: (1/β) * (1/Z)∑ e_i β ∂c_i = ∑ p_i ∂c_i. So p weights; no derivative of p. Good. For p_soft, gradient exact. For active mix, p changes discontinuously; ignore derivative. For SLSQP, use p_soft only to be smooth. Use active_mix=0. Then objective `lse_val`. Good. Use mixed weights in our mirror for heuristic.

### LP implementation top-level
```
def _lp_direction(a, M, ub=100.0):
   if not HAVE_SCIPY or a.size==0: return None
   n=a.size
   if n>700: return None
   data = np.repeat(np.asarray(a,dtype=float), n)
   cols = np.repeat(np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32), n)
   offs = np.tile(np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32), n)
   rows = (cols + offs).astype(np.int32)
   A = _sp_sparse.csr_matrix((data,(rows,cols)), shape=(2*n-1,n))
   A.sum_duplicates()
   b = np.full(2*n-1, M, dtype=float)
   c = -np.ones(n)
   bounds = [(0.0, ub)]*n
   try:
      res=_sp_opt.linprog(c, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve':True, 'dual_feasibility_tolerance':1e-9})
   except Exception:
      return None
   if res is None or not hasattr(res,'x') or not np.all(np.isfinite(res.x)):
      return None
   if res.status not in (0,2): # 0 optimal, 2? not better. maybe allow 0 only
      if res.status==0: pass else return None
   g=np.maximum(res.x,0)
   s=g.sum()
   if s <= 1e-12: return None
   return g/s
```
HiGHS status: 0 optimal. Some `res.status == 0`.

Or use `time_limit` if HiGHS supports. SciPy HiGHS options include `time_limit`. Let's use `options={'presolve': True, 'time_limit': 20.0}` maybe. If unsupported exception catch. But if exception due unknown option, we can catch and retry without time. To keep simple, no time. But risk long block. n≤300 599 constraints, fine.

```
def _try_perturb(a, best, rng, strength):
   r=rng.random()
   if r<0.4:
      a = a * np.exp(rng.normal(0,strength,a.size)); if rng.random()<0.2: a += 0.05*rng.normal(0,0.1,a.size)? no negative
```

```
def _get_given_sequence():
    seq = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if seq is None: return None
    try: arr=np.asarray(seq,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except: return None
    if arr.size==0: return None
    arr=np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr=np.maximum(arr,0)
    if arr.sum() <=0: return None
    return arr
```

#### `_score`
```
def _score(a):
    a=_norm(a)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return 1e100
    c=_conv(a)
    mb=float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(mb): return 1e100
    return 2.0*float(n)*mb
```

```
def _lp_improve(a, seconds, rng):
   start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+seconds; a=_norm(a); best=a; best_score=_score(a)
   n=a.size
   if not HAVE_SCIPY or n>400 or deadline-start<10: return ...
   c=_conv(a); M=float(c.max())
   for factor in (1.0,0.85,0.7):
      if time>deadline-5 break
      # Maybe first 1.0 no improvement; try lower
      g=_lp_direction(a, M*factor)
      if g is None: continue
      for t in (1.0,0.6,0.3,0.1):
         cand=_norm((1-t)*a + t*g)
         sc=_score(cand)
         if sc < best_score: ...
      # also optimize g a bit? no time.
   return best,best_score
```

#### `_normalize` repeated with `np.nan_to_num`: In inner optimize, calling `_normalize` with np.asarray and `np.where` finite every time maybe overhead. Use fast version after ensuring finite. But for simplicity okay for n up to 4096, iterations many maybe overhead. Let's make `_normalize` efficient:
```
def _normalize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        a = np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64) / float(a.size)
    return a / s
```

```
def _norm_fast(a):
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s <= 1e-300: return np.ones(a.size)/a.size
    return a/s
```

#### `_mix_weights` uses `p_soft` maybe; if active_mix 0, still adds base uniform. Implementation:
```
def _mix_weights(c, p_soft, active_rho, active_mix):
    m = c.size
    p = np.array(p_soft, copy=True)
    if active_mix > 0.0 and active_rho > 0.0:
       maxc = float(c.max()); minc = float(c.min())
       rng_c = maxc - minc
       if rng_c > 0:
          thresh = maxc - active_rho*rng_c
          active = c >= thresh
          cnt = int(active.sum())
          if cnt > 1:
             p_active = np.zeros(m)
             p_active[active]=1.0/cnt
             p = (1.0-active_mix)*p + active_mix*p_active
    base = 1e-6
    p = (1.0 - base)*p + base/m
    s=p.sum()
    if s > 0: p = p/s
    return p
```
If active_mix >1? no.

#### `_optimize_once` uses `_norm_fast`. After perturbation, ensure np ndarray.
`_perturb_mix` returns normalized._theme
```
def _perturb_mix(a, rng, sigma):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    r=float(rng.random())
    n=a.size
    if r < 0.45:
       w=max(2, n//32)
       start=int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-w+1)))
       tmp=a.copy(); tmp[start:start+w] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
       tmp=np.maximum(tmp,0)
       return _norm_fast(tmp)
    elif r < 0.8:
       noise=rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n)
       tmp=a*np.exp(noise)
       return _norm_fast(tmp)
    else:
       b=_make_rand_smooth(n, rng, 0.5, 8)
       tmp=0.7*a + 0.3*b
       return _norm_fast(np.maximum(tmp,0))
```
No sym.

#### `_make_rand_smooth` uses `_norm` or `_norm_fast`; result finite.
```
def _make_rand_smooth(n, rng, amp, cutoff):
   n=max(int(n),2)
   z=rng.normal(0,1,n)
   F=np.fft.rfft(z)
   cut=max(2, len(F)//max(1,int(cutoff)))
   if cut < len(F): F[cut:] = 0.0
   f=np.fft.irfft(F, n)
   f = f - float(np.mean(f))
   sd=float(np.std(f))
   if sd > 1e-12: f=f/sd
   a=1.0 + float(amp)*f
   a=np.maximum(a,1e-8)
   return _norm(a)
```
If cutoff small (2), cut = len(F)//2? Wait `cutoff` parameter meaning divisor. If cutoff=2, keep len/2 (low half); if cutoff=32, keep len/32. For smooth. Good.
Maybe for random rough use cutoff=1? We can pass 1. But keep.

#### `_make_exp`
```
def _make_exp(n, alpha, flip=False):
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   if flip: a=np.exp(-alpha*(1-x))
   else: a=np.exp(-alpha*x)
   a += 1e-6
   return _norm(a)
```

```
def _make_sin(n, amp, freq, phase, square=False):
   x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   a=1+amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*(freq*x+phase))
   if square: a=a*a
   a=np.maximum(a,1e-6); return _norm(a)
```

#### `_resample`
Need `mode` can int/float. We can use:
```
def _resample(old, n, mode=0):
   old=_norm(old); n=int(n)
   if old.size == n: return old.copy()
   x_old=np.linspace(0,1,old.size)
   x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   mode = int(mode) % 3
   if mode == 0:
      y=np.interp(x_new,x_old,old)
   elif mode == 1:
      y=np.exp(np.interp(x_new,x_old,np.log(old+1e-10)))
   else:
      # cosine taper edges? y = interp * edge? Maybe use average of old and exp?
      y=np.interp(x_new,x_old,old)
      y = y * (np.cos(np.pi*(np.linspace(0,1,n)))[None? no] **0.1?)
   return _norm(np.maximum(y,1e-12))
```
Mode 2 maybe interpolation of squared to enhance peaks.
```
or mode1 log; mode2 interp of old^0.5?
```
Keep simple.

### In `_make_seeds`, edge seeds may be many and time. We can limit counts.
Let's write:
```
def _make_seeds(n, rng, given, best):
    n=int(n); pre=[]
    pre.append(_make_uniform(n))
    for alpha in ...: pre.append exp left/right; mixture
    for slope ...
    for amp in (0.25,0.5,0.8): freq (1,2): phase (0,0.25,0.5): square False,True? That double. Maybe 3*2*3*2=36. okay.
    for amp in (0.3,0.6,1.0): pre.append rand_smooth cutoff 16; pre.append cutoff 64
    # edge
    for C in (0.6,0.8,1.0,1.3,1.8,2.5):
       for lf in (0.0,0.5,1.0):
          for rf in (0.0,0.5,1.0):
             for mid in (0.0,0.3,0.8):
                pre.append(_make_edge_seed(n,C,lf,rf,mid))
    # sparse
    for f in (0.7,1.0,1.4,2.0,3.0):
       m=int(f*np.sqrt(n)); m=min(max(2,m),n-1)
       pre.append(_make_sparse(n,m,rng))
       pre.append(_make_sparse(n,m,rng,0.25))
    # cap pre
    if len(pre) > 120:
       keep=pre[:30]
       rest=pre[30:]
       if len(rest) > 90:
          idx=rng.choice(len(rest), 90, replace=False)
          rest=[rest[i] for i in idx]
       pre=keep+rest
    seeds=pre
    if given is not None and given.size > 4:
       for md in (0,1,2):
          seeds.append(_resample(given,n,md))
    if best is not None and best.size > 4:
       for md in (0,1,2):
          seeds.append(_resample(best,n,md))
       # mix best with exp
       r=_resample(best,n,0)
       e=_make_exp(n,1.0)
       seeds.append(_norm(0.8*r+0.2*e))
    # final cap 160 keeping last 12
    if len(seeds) > 160:
       key=seeds[-12:]
       rem=seeds[:-12]
       if len(rem) > 148:
          idx=rng.choice(len(rem),148,replace=False)
          rem=[rem[i] for i in idx]
       seeds=rem+key
    return seeds
```

```
"""
Search low-peak nonnegative sequences a*a ...
"""
import numpy as np
import time
import math
try:
    from scipy import optimize as _sp_opt
    from scipy import sparse as _sp_sp
    try:
        from scipy.fft import next_fast_len as _next_fast_len
    except Exception:
        _next_fast_len = None
    HAVE_SCIPY = True
except Exception:
    _sp_opt = None
    _sp_sp = None
    _next_fast_len = None
    HAVE_SCIPY = False

_SLS = {"bfft":1,"wfft":1,"active_rho":0.15,"active_mix":0.0}

def _next_pow2(size):
    size=int(size)
    if size <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (size - 1).bit_length()

def _fft_len(size):
    size=int(size)
    if size <=1: return 1
    if _next_fast_len is not None:
        try:
            L=int(_next_fast_len(size))
            if L >= size: return L
        except Exception:
            pass
    return _next_pow2(size)
```
Note Python bit length: if size=5, (size-1).bit_length()=3, 1<<3=8; if size=4 -> (3).bit_length=2, 1<<2=4; size=1 returns1. Good.

normalize:
```
def _normalize(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size ==0: return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.isfinite(a).all():
        a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)/float(a.size)
    return a/s
```
`np.isfinite(a).all()` returns numpy bool; okay.
Norm fast:
```
def _norm_fast(a):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if s <= 1e-300: return np.ones(n,dtype=np.float64)/float(n)
    return a/s
```
This scans a again in np.maximum. In optimizer a already ndarray positive. Could assume and skip max? But for safety. It allocates a new array. Fine.

_conv:
```
def _conv(a, fft_len=None):
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return np.array([0.0])
    m=2*n-1
    if fft_len is None: fft_len=_fft_len(m)
    A=np.fft.rfft(a, fft_len)
    b=np.fft.irfft(A*A, fft_len)
    b=b[:m]
    return np.maximum(b,0.0)
```
_score:
```
def _score(a):
    a=_normalize(a)
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return 1e100
    c=_conv(a)
    mb=float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(mb) or mb <=0: return 1e100 if mb<0 else 0.0? If mb=0, score0 impossible but if all zero? normalized not.
    return 2.0*float(n)*mb
```
If mb=0 return 0.0, but normalized sum1 gives mb>0. Use `return 2*...` if finite.

_lse:
```
def _lse_p(c,beta):
    cmax=float(c.max())
    m=c.size
    if m ==0 or cmax <=0: return np.ones(m)/max(m,1), cmax
    beta=float(beta)
    if beta <=0: return np.full(m,1.0/m), cmax
    beta=min(1.0e7,beta)
    x=beta*(c-cmax)
    x=np.clip(x,-745.0,0.0)
    e=np.exp(x)
    s=float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <=0:
        p=np.full(m,1.0/m); return p,cmax
    p=e/s
    val=cmax + math.log(s)/beta
    return p,val
```
_lse_val could call and return val, but to avoid duplicate, in optimizer use `p_soft,lse_old = _lse_p`.

_mix:
```
def _mix_weights(c,p_soft,active_rho,active_mix):
    m=c.size
    p=np.array(p_soft, copy=True)
    if active_mix >0 and active_rho >0:
        maxc=float(c.max()); minc=float(c.min())
        rng=maxc-minc
        if rng >0:
            thresh=maxc - float(active_rho)*rng
            active = c >= thresh
            cnt=int(np.count_nonzero(active))
            if cnt > 1:
               pa=np.zeros(m)
               pa[active]=1.0/cnt
               p=(1.0-float(active_mix))*p + float(active_mix)*pa
    base=1e-6
    p=(1.0-base)*p + (base/m)
    s=float(p.sum())
    if s <=0: p=np.full(m,1.0/m)
    else: p=p/s
    return p
```
If m=0, but c length >0.

_grad:
```
def _grad(a,weights,fft_len=None):
    n=a.size
    if fft_len is None: fft_len=_fft_len(3*n-2)
    ar=a[::-1]
    A=np.fft.rfft(ar, fft_len)
    W=np.fft.rfft(weights, fft_len)
    s=np.fft.irfft(A*W, fft_len)
    g=s[n-1:2*n-1]
    g=np.maximum(g,0.0)
    return 2.0*g
```

```
def _make_uniform(n):
    n=max(int(n),2)
    return np.ones(n,dtype=np.float64)/float(n)
```

```
def _make_exp(n, alpha, flip=False):
    n=max(int(n),2)
    x=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    if flip: a=np.exp(-float(alpha)*(1.0-x))
    else: a=np.exp(-float(alpha)*x)
    a=np.maximum(a,1e-9)
    return _norm_fast(a)
```

#### `_edge_profile` optimized with local variables
```
def _edge_profile(n, C, q0_frac):
    n=max(int(n),2)
    q=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if C <=0: return q
    M=float(C)/float(n)
    sqrtM=math.sqrt(M)
    q0=float(q0_frac)*sqrtM
    q[0]=q0
    max_len=min(n, 4*int(math.sqrt(n))+120)
    q0sq=q0*q0
    for k in range(1,max_len):
        if k == 1:
            s=0.0
        else:
            seg=q[1:k]
            if seg.size:
                s=2.0*float(np.dot(seg, seg[::-1]))
            else: s=0.0
        disc=q0sq + M - s
        if disc <=0:
            break
        val=-q0 + math.sqrt(disc)
        if val <= 1e-15:
            break
        q[k]=val
    return q
```
If q0=0, q[0]=0. For k=1 val=√M. Good. If q0_frac=0, q[0]=0, max profile length 2? k=2 breaks. Good.
Could profile sum raw perhaps low if C small; okay.

`_make_edge_seed`:
```
def _make_edge_seed(n,C,lf,rf,mid):
    n=max(int(n),2)
    L=_edge_profile(n,C,lf)
    R=_edge_profile(n,C,rf)
    a=np.zeros(n)
    cl=int(np.count_nonzero(L)); cr=int(np.count_nonzero(R))
    if cl: a[:cl]=L
    if cr: a[n-cr:]=R[::-1]
    if mid > 0:
       a += float(mid) * (float(C)/float(n))
    a += 1e-10 * (float(C)/float(n) + 1e-12)
    return _norm_fast(a)
```
If profile zeros (q0=0 length1? q[0]=0 counted zero). Good.

`_make_sparse` uses `rng.choice` maybe if m > n? cap.
```
def _make_sparse(n,m,rng,edge_frac=0.0):
   n=max(int(n),2); m=int(m)
   if m <=0: return _make_uniform(n)
   m=min(m, n)
   a=np.zeros(n)
   if edge_frac >0.0:
       L=max(2,int(n*float(edge_frac)))
       m1=max(1,m//2); m2=m-m1
       left=np.unique(np.asarray(rng.integers(0,L,size=m1), dtype=int))
       right=n-1-np.unique(np.asarray(rng.integers(0,L,size=m2), dtype=int))
       support=np.unique(np.concatenate((left,right)))
   else:
       support=np.asarray(rng.choice(n, size=min(m,n), replace=False), dtype=int)
   if support.size ==0: return _make_uniform(n)
   if float(rng.random()) < 0.5:
       w=rng.dirichlet(np.ones(support.size))
       a[support]=w
   else:
       a[support]=1.0
   a += 1e-9
   return _norm_fast(a)
```
If `rng.integers` returns ndarray already. np.asarray okay.

`_resample`:
```
def _resample(old,n,mode=0):
   old=_normalize(old)
   n=max(int(n),2)
   if old.size == n: return old.copy()
   x_old=np.linspace(0,1,old.size)
   x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   mode=int(mode)%3
   if mode == 0:
      y=np.interp(x_new,x_old,old)
   elif mode == 1:
      y=np.exp(np.interp(x_new,x_old,np.log(old+1e-10)))
   else:
      y=np.interp(x_new,x_old,np.sqrt(np.maximum(old,0.0)))
      y=y*y
   return _norm_fast(np.maximum(y,1e-12))
```
Mode2 sqrt interp then square approximates peaks.

`_make_seeds` as above. Need call `_norm_fast` not `_normalize` in adding. `_make_uniform` returns. For given/best, `_resample` returns norm.

### `_optimize_once` implement exactly.
Need function `_try_perturb` top-level.

```
def _perturb_mix(a,rng,sigma):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n=a.size
    if n==0: return _make_uniform(8)
    r=float(rng.random())
    sigma=float(sigma)
    if r < 0.45:
       w=max(2, n//32)
       start=int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n-w+1)))
       tmp=a.copy()
       tmp[start:start+w] *= float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma)))
       return _norm_fast(np.maximum(tmp,0.0))
    elif r < 0.8:
       tmp=a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
       return _norm_fast(np.maximum(tmp,0.0))
    else:
       b=_make_rand_smooth(n, rng, 0.5, 8)
       tmp=0.7*a + 0.3*b
       return _norm_fast(np.maximum(tmp,0.0))
```
`rng.integers(0, max(1,n-w+1))` if high=int. Good.

`_optimize_once`:
```
def _optimize_once(a, seconds, rng, profile=0):
    start=time.monotonic()
    deadline=start+max(0.0,float(seconds))
    if a is None:
       a=_make_uniform(256)
    a=_norm_fast(np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel())
    n=a.size
    if n < 8:
       a=_make_uniform(64); n=64
    if n > 16384:
       a=_resample(a,8192,0); n=8192
    bfft=_fft_len(2*n-1); wfft=_fft_len(3*n-2)
    c=_conv(a,bfft)
    maxc=float(c.max())
    if maxc <=0: maxc=1e-300
    score=2.0*float(n)*maxc
    best=a.copy(); best_score=score
    # profile parameters
    if profile % 3 == 0:
        beta_start=4.0; beta_end=28.0; arho=0.22; amix=0.30; eta0=0.35
    elif profile % 3 == 1:
        beta_start=10.0; beta_end=70.0; arho=0.07; amix=0.12; eta0=0.25
    else:
        beta_start=3.0; beta_end=16.0; arho=0.32; amix=0.45; eta0=0.5
    beta=beta_start; eta=eta0
    fail=0; it=0; last_lse=0; last_hard=0
    p_soft,lse_now=_lse_p(c,beta/maxc if maxc>0 else 1.0)
    best_lse=float(lse_now)
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if maxc <=0: maxc=1e-300
        beta_use=min(1.0e7, beta/maxc)
        p_soft,lse_old=_lse_p(c,beta_use)
        if lse_old < best_lse - 1e-14:
             best_lse=float(lse_old); last_lse=it
        p=_mix_weights(c,p_soft,arho,amix)
        grad=_grad(a,p,wfft)
        mu=float(np.dot(a,grad))
        h=grad - a*mu
        absh=np.abs(h)
        scale=float(np.max(absh)) if h.size else 0.0
        if (not np.isfinite(scale)) or scale < 1e-14:
             a=_perturb_mix(best,rng,0.3)
             c=_conv(a,bfft); maxc=float(c.max()); score=2.0*float(n)*maxc
             beta=max(beta_start,beta*0.7); eta=eta0; fail=0
             it+=1
             continue
        hs=h/scale
        accepted=False
        for mult in (1.0,0.5):
            eff=eta*float(mult)
            step=np.clip(-eff*hs,-4.0,4.0)
            new_a=a*np.exp(step)
            new_a=_norm_fast(new_a)
            new_c=_conv(new_a,bfft)
            new_mc=float(new_c.max())
            if new_mc <=0: new_mc=1e-300
            new_score=2.0*float(n)*new_mc
            _, new_lse = _lse_p(new_c,beta_use)
            hard = (new_score < score - 1e-12) or (new_score < best_score - 1e-14)
            smooth = new_lse < lse_old - 1e-14
            if hard or smooth:
                 accepted=True
                 a=new_a; c=new_c; maxc=new_mc; score=new_score
                 # if mult <1, don't increase eta quite
                 if mult == 1.0:
                     eta=min(2.0, eta*1.08)
                 else:
                     eta=min(1.2, eta*1.08) # actually mult used 0.5, new effective eta? We should set eta to eta*mult*1.08? Wait if first attempt with eta rejected, second with eta/2 accepted, to keep similar try set eta = min(2, eta*0.5*1.08). We can store mult_accept=mult.
                 break
        if accepted:
            fail=0
            if new_score < best_score - 1e-14:
                best=a.copy(); best_score=new_score; last_hard=it
            if new_lse < best_lse - 1e-14:
                best_lse=new_lse; last_lse=it
            # new_lse variable defined only if at least one candidate computed; yes.
        else:
            eta *= 0.5
            fail += 1
        # Beta anneal
        if it % 40 == 0:
            if beta < beta_end:
                beta = min(beta_end, beta * 1.04)
            elif beta > beta_start:
                beta = max(beta_start, beta * 0.95)
        if it % 200 == 0:
            p_soft_tmp,lse_reset = _lse_p(c,beta/maxc if maxc>0 else 1.0)
            if lse_reset > best_lse:
                best_lse = float(lse_reset); last_lse=it
        if (it > 80 and it - last_lse > 700 and it - last_hard > 700) or fail > 600:
            a=_perturb_mix(best,rng,0.35 if fail>200 else 0.2)
            c=_conv(a,bfft); maxc=float(c.max()); score=2.0*float(n)*maxc
            eta=eta0
            fail=0
            beta=max(beta_start, beta*0.6)
            p_soft_tmp,lse_reset=_lse_p(c,max(1.0, beta/maxc if maxc>0 else 1.0))
            best_lse=float(lse_reset); last_lse=it
        if score > best_score * 1.25 + 1e-8 and fail > 100:
            a=best.copy()
            c=_conv(a,bfft); maxc=float(c.max()); score=2.0*float(n)*maxc
            eta=eta0; fail=0
        it += 1
    return best, best_score
```

### SLSQP functions
```
def _sls_sum(x): return float(np.sum(x)-1.0)
def _sls_objective(x,beta):
    a=_norm_fast(np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64).ravel())
    c=_conv(a,_SLS['bfft'])
    maxc=float(c.max())
    if maxc <=0 or not np.isfinite(maxc): return 1e100
    p_soft,val=_lse_p(c, min(1e7, float(beta)/maxc))
    return float(val)
def _sls_gradient(x,beta):
    a=_norm_fast(np.asarray(x,dtype=np.float64).ravel())
    c=_conv(a,_SLS['bfft'])
    maxc=float(c.max())
    if maxc <=0: return np.zeros_like(a)
    p_soft,val=_lse_p(c,min(1e7,float(beta)/maxc))
    g=_grad(a,p_soft,_SLS['wfft'])
    return g - float(np.mean(g))
```
`_SLS['bfft']` int. In polish set.
`_sls_objective` ignoring active; smooth.

`_polish_slsqp`:
```
def _polish_slsqp(a, seconds, rng):
    a=_normalize(a); n=a.size
    best=a.copy(); best_score=_score(a)
    if not HAVE_SCIPY or n <8 or n>512 or seconds <20: return best,best_score
    deadline=time.monotonic()+seconds
    _SLS['bfft']=_fft_len(2*n-1); _SLS['wfft']=_fft_len(3*n-2)
    bounds=[(0.0,1.0)]*n
    constraints=[{"type":"eq","fun":_sls_sum}]
    betas=(3.0,6.0,12.0,24.0)
    for bi,beta in enumerate(betas):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5: break
       try:
          res=_sp_opt.minimize(_sls_objective, a, jac=_sls_gradient, method='SLSQP',
                               args=(float(beta),), bounds=bounds, constraints=constraints,
                               options={'maxiter':80, 'ftol':1e-10, 'disp':False})
          if res is not None and hasattr(res,'x'):
             cand=_normalize(res.x)
             sc=_score(cand)
             if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score - 1e-15:
                 best=cand; best_score=sc; a=cand
       except Exception:
          pass
    return best,best_score
```
Note residuals: SLSQP may be slow; 80 iters. Good.

```
def _lp_direction(a, M):
    if not HAVE_SCIPY or a.size ==0: return None
    n=a.size
    if n>350: return None
    if M <=0 or not np.isfinite(M): return None
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    length=n*n
    cols=np.repeat(np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32), n)
    offs=np.tile(np.arange(n, dtype=np.int32), n)
    rows=(cols+offs).astype(np.int32)
    data=np.repeat(a, n)
    A=_sp_sp.csr_matrix((data,(rows,cols)), shape=(2*n-1,n))
    try: A.sum_duplicates()
    except: pass
    b=np.full(2*n-1, float(M), dtype=np.float64)
    cvec=-np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    bounds=[(0.0,1000.0)]*n
    res=None
    try:
       res=_sp_opt.linprog(cvec, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve':True})
    except Exception:
       try:
          res=_sp_opt.linprog(cvec, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
       except Exception:
          try:
             res=_sp_opt.linprog(cvec, A_ub=np.asarray(A.todense()), b_ub=b, bounds=bounds)
          except Exception:
             return None
    if res is None or not hasattr(res,'x') or res.x is None: return None
    if getattr(res,'status',99) !=0: return None
    g=np.asarray(res.x,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if g.size != n: return None
    g=np.maximum(g,0.0)
    s=float(g.sum())
    if s <=1e-12: return None
    return g/s
```
Status can be 0; maybe 2 acceptable? No. If objective unbounded but status? skip.

```
def _lp_improve(a, seconds, rng):
    a=_normalize(a); n=a.size
    best=a.copy(); best_score=_score(a)
    if not HAVE_SCIPY or n>350 or seconds <15: return best,best_score
    deadline=time.monotonic()+seconds
    c=_conv(a)
    M=float(c.max())
    if M <=0: return ...
    for factor in (1.0,0.85,0.7):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 10: break
       g=_lp_direction(a, M*float(factor))
       if g is None: continue
       candidates=[]
       for t in (1.0,0.7,0.4,0.2):
           cand=_norm_fast((1.0-float(t))*a + float(t)*g)
           candidates.append(cand)
       # also g plus best
       for cand in candidates:
           sc=_score(cand)
           if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
               best=cand; best_score=sc
    return best,best_score
```

```
def _get_given_sequence():
    seq = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if seq is None: return None
    try:
       arr=np.asarray(seq,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
       return None
    if arr.size ==0: return None
    arr=np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr=np.maximum(arr,0.0)
    if arr.sum() <=0: return None
    return arr
```

```
def _finalize_list(a):
    a=_normalize(a)
    if a.size > 8192: a=_resample(a,4096,0)
    a=np.nan_to_num(a, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    a=np.maximum(a,0.0)
    s=float(a.sum())
    if s <=0: a=np.ones(a.size)/a.size; s=1
    else: a=a/s
    return [float(x) for x in a]
```

```
def _choose_final(candidates):
    eval_fun=globals().get("evaluate_sequence", None)
    best_list=None; best_val=np.inf
    used_lengths=set()
    for a in candidates:
       if a is None: continue
       lst=_finalize_list(a)
       if not lst: continue
       val=np.inf
       if callable(eval_fun):
           try:
              ev=eval_fun(lst)
              ev=float(ev)
              if np.isfinite(ev): val=ev
           except Exception:
              pass
       if not np.isfinite(val):
           valid=np.asarray(lst)
           val=_score(valid)
       if val < best_val:
           best_val=val; best_list=lst
    if best_list is None:
       best_list=_finalize_list(_make_exp(1024,1.0))
    return best_list
```

```
def propose_candidate(seed=0, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    t0=time.monotonic()
    try: budget=float(budget_s)
    except: budget=2400.0
    if not np.isfinite(budget) or budget <= 0: budget=2400.0
    budget=max(30.0, min(2400.0, budget))
    deadline=t0+budget-45.0
    if deadline < t0+60: deadline=t0+max(60,budget)-10
    try: rng=np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    except: rng=np.random.default_rng(0)
    given=_get_given_sequence()
    best_a=None; best_score=np.inf
    # initial quick
    for n in (128,256,512,1024):
       for flip in (False,True):
          a=_make_exp(n,1.0,flip); sc=_score(a); update
       a=_make_sin(n,0.5,1,0.25); update
    if given is not None:
       a=_normalize(given); sc=_score(a); update; print
    scale=min(1.0, max(0.05, budget/2400.0))
    configs=[(256,6,320),(512,6,340),(1024,5,400),(2048,4,420),(4096,4,480)]
    for n, top_target, sec_total in configs:
       sec_total=int(sec_total*scale)
       min_per=10 if n<=512 else 15
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - max(30, sec_total//max(1,top_target)):
           break
       if sec_total < 60:
           continue
       # if already below 1.49 and n large? break? maybe.
       seeds=_make_seeds(n, rng, given, best_a)
       if not seeds: continue
       scored=[]
       for a in seeds:
           a=_normalize(a)
           sc=_score(a)
           if np.isfinite(sc):
              # prune extremely bad? if best_score finite and sc > best_score*1.8+0.5: maybe skip
              if np.isfinite(best_score) and sc > best_score + 0.6:
                  continue
              scored.append((sc,a))
       if not scored: continue
       scored.sort(key=_pair_first)
       top=min(top_target, len(scored))
       per=sec_total/float(top)
       for rank in range(top):
           sc0,a0=scored[rank]
           t_left=deadline - time.monotonic()
           if t_left < 8: break
           if np.isfinite(best_score) and sc0 > best_score + 0.1:
              allowed=min(per, 12.0*scale + 5.0)
           elif np.isfinite(best_score) and sc0 > best_score:
              allowed=min(per, 20.0*scale + 5.0)
           else:

           allowed=max(5.0, min(allowed, t_left-2.0))
           if allowed <= 1.0: continue
           profile=rank % 3

           except Exception:
              continue
           if np.isfinite(rs) and rs < best_score:
              best_a=ra; best_score=rs
              print(f"n={best_a.size} score={best_score:.8f} t={time.monotonic()-t0:.1f}s", flush=True)
           # optional polish small n
           if n <= 350 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 25:
              try:
                 la, ls = _lp_improve(ra if np.isfinite(rs) else a0, 25.0*scale+10.0, rng)
              except Exception: la,ls=a0,sc0
              if ls < best_score: update
           if n <= 512 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 30:
              try:
                 sa, ss = _polish_slsqp(best_a if best_a is not None else a0, 25.0*scale+10.0, rng)
              except: pass
              if ss < best_score: update
    # optional 8192
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 120 and best_score > 1.49 and scale >0.5:
        n=8192; seeds=_make_seeds(n,rng,given,best_a); scored... top=2; per= min(120, (deadline-time-30)/2)
        similar
    # final polish
    if best_a is None: best_a=_make_exp(1024,1.0); best_score=_score(best_a)
    left=deadline - time.monotonic()
    if left > 30:
       try:
          ra,rs=_optimize_once(best_a, max(5.0,left-5.0), rng, profile=1)
          if rs<best_score: update
       except: pass
    if best_score < 1.5027:
       print("target beaten", best_score, flush=True)
    candidates=[best_a]
    if given is not None: candidates.append(given)
    if best_a is not None and best_a.size > 1024:
       candidates.append(_resample(best_a, 2048 if best_a.size<=4096 else 1024, 0))
       candidates.append(_resample(best_a, 1024, 0))
    candidates.append(_make_exp(1024,1.0))
    final=_choose_final(candidates)
    return final
```
